	The proof identifies many disjoint blocks of rotated mode pairs on which the embedded ground state $\mathsf{J}\ket{\Omega_{F}}$ remains close to the corresponding bosonic reference state.
	On each such block, we project locally onto total occupation one on each side; the blocks will be chosen so that this event has a uniformly positive probability for the reference state.
	The resulting entanglement lower bounds can then be summed using strong superadditivity of squashed entanglement.
	
	Since $s_{k}/2\le r_{k}\le s_{k}$ for $s_{k}\le1/2$, and since $W=\sum_{k}s_{k}^{2}$, set
	\begin{align}
		T:=\sum_{k}\zeta_{k} = \sum_{k} r_{k}^{2}.
	\end{align}
	Then $T\ge W/4$.
	Moreover, Lemma~\ref{lem:sharpness-matrix} gives $W\ge1/(4D^{2\alpha})$ and $f=o(1)$, while $\zeta_{k}=r_{k}^{2}\le s_{k}^{2}\le f^{2}$.
	Therefore,
	\begin{align}
		T\ge\frac{W}{4}\ge\frac{1}{16D^{2\alpha}},\qquad
		\max_{k}\zeta_{k}\le f^{2}=o(1). \label{eq:sharpness-total-squeezing-weight}
	\end{align}
	Set $u_{*}:=1/(64D^{2\alpha})$.
	Then $T\ge4u_{*}$, while Eq.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-total-squeezing-weight} implies $\max_{k}\zeta_{k}<u_{*}$ for all sufficiently large $\ell_{\Lambda}$.
	Starting from any nonzero $\zeta_{k}$ and adding further nonzero terms until their sum first reaches $u_{*}$ therefore produces an index set $I_{j}$ whose total weight lies between $u_{*}$ and $2u_{*}$.
	Repeating this procedure with the remaining indices gives disjoint index sets $I_{j}$ satisfying
	\begin{align}
		u_{*}\le u_{j}:=\sum_{k\in I_{j}}\zeta_{k}\le2u_{*}, \label{eq:sharpness-mode-block-weight}
	\end{align}
	with a remainder of total weight less than $u_{*}$.
	If $m$ is the number of index sets $I_{j}$ constructed above, their total weight is at most $2mu_{*}$, so $T<2mu_{*}+u_{*}$.
	Since $T\ge4u_{*}$, this gives
	\begin{align}
		m\ge\frac{T}{4u_{*}}\ge\frac{W}{16u_{*}}. \label{eq:sharpness-number-mode-blocks}
	\end{align}
	
	
	After embedding the spin ground state into the bosonic Fock space by $\mathsf{J}$, let $\rho_{j}$ denote the reduced state on the rotated mode pairs $(\widehat{a}_{k},\widehat{b}_{k})$ with $k\in I_{j}$, obtained by tracing out all rotated modes with $k\notin I_{j}$.
	Define
	\begin{align}
		\mathfrak{d}_{j}:=\sum_{k\in I_{j}}\operatorname{Tr}(\rho_{j}Q_{k}^{\mathrm{b}}),\qquad
		Q_{k}^{\mathrm{b}}:=\mathds{1}-\ket{\phi_{k}}\bra{\phi_{k}}.
	\end{align}
	Each term $\operatorname{Tr}(\rho_{j}Q_{k}^{\mathrm{b}})$ measures the weight outside the reference state $\ket{\phi_{k}}$ for the $k$th mode pair, so $\mathfrak{d}_{j}$ quantifies the total deviation from the corresponding bosonic reference states over the indices in $I_{j}$.
	Since the index sets $I_{j}$ are disjoint and $\rho_{j}$ is the corresponding reduced state of $\mathsf{J}\ket{\Omega_{F}}$, summing over $j$ gives a sub-sum of the total defect in Eq.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-defect-projectors}.
	Hence,
	\begin{align}
		\sum_{j}\mathfrak{d}_{j}\le3f^{2}W. \label{eq:sharpness-total-index-set-defect}
	\end{align}
	We choose $\vartheta:=(D-\alpha)/(24D)$ and $\mathfrak{d}_{*}:=(u_{*}\vartheta/8)^{2}$, and call an index set $I_{j}$ good when $\mathfrak{d}_{j}\le\mathfrak{d}_{*}$.
	Since every index set that is not good contributes more than $\mathfrak{d}_{*}$ to Eq.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-total-index-set-defect}, the number of such index sets is at most $3f^{2}W/\mathfrak{d}_{*}$.
	Moreover, $u_{*}$ and $\mathfrak{d}_{*}$ are fixed positive constants, whereas $f^{2}=o(1)$.
	Thus, for all sufficiently large $\ell_{\Lambda}$,
	\begin{align}
		\#\{j:\mathfrak{d}_{j}>\mathfrak{d}_{*}\}
		\le\frac{3f^{2}W}{\mathfrak{d}_{*}}
		\le\frac{W}{32u_{*}}.
	\end{align}
	Combining this with Eq.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-number-mode-blocks}, there are therefore at least $W/(32u_{*})$ good index sets.
	
	
	Fix one good index set $I_{j}$ and write $M_{j}:=|I_{j}|$ and $\ket{\Phi_{j}}:=\bigotimes_{k\in I_{j}}\ket{\phi_{k}}$.
	Since $Q_{k}^{\mathrm{b}}=\mathds{1}-\ket{\phi_{k}}\bra{\phi_{k}}$ acts on the $k$th mode pair, one has $\ket{\Phi_{j}}\bra{\Phi_{j}}=\prod_{k\in I_{j}}(\mathds{1}-Q_{k}^{\mathrm{b}})$.
	The projectors commute, so $\mathds{1}-\ket{\Phi_{j}}\bra{\Phi_{j}}\le\sum_{k\in I_{j}}Q_{k}^{\mathrm{b}}$, which gives $1-\bra{\Phi_{j}}\rho_{j}\ket{\Phi_{j}}\le\mathfrak{d}_{j}$.
	Using the trace-distance bound against a pure state, we therefore obtain
	\begin{align}
		\frac{1}{2}\left \|  \rho_{j}-\ket{\Phi_{j}}\bra{\Phi_{j}} \right \|_{1}
		\le\sqrt{1-\bra{\Phi_{j}}\rho_{j}\ket{\Phi_{j}}}
		\le\sqrt{\mathfrak{d}_{j}}. \label{eq:sharpness-good-block-distance}
	\end{align}
	
	
	Let $\ket{k}_{A}$ denote the Fock state with one boson in the rotated mode $\widehat{a}_{k}$ and zero bosons in all other modes $\widehat{a}_{\ell}$ with $\ell\in I_{j}$, and define $\ket{k}_{B}$ analogously for the modes $\widehat{b}_{k}$.
	Let
	\begin{align}
		\Pi_{A,j}^{(1)}:=\sum_{k\in I_{j}}\ket{k}_{A}\bra{k}_{A},\qquad
		\Pi_{B,j}^{(1)}:=\sum_{k\in I_{j}}\ket{k}_{B}\bra{k}_{B},\qquad
		\Pi_{j}^{\mathrm{succ}}:=\Pi_{A,j}^{(1)}\otimes\Pi_{B,j}^{(1)}.
	\end{align}
	Thus $\Pi_{j}^{\mathrm{succ}}$ selects the sector in which the modes indexed by $I_{j}$ contain exactly one boson on each side.
	For the reference state $\ket{\Phi_{j}}=\bigotimes_{k\in I_{j}}\ket{\phi_{k}}$, Eq.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-bosonic-ground-state} shows that the projection $\Pi_{j}^{\mathrm{succ}}$ retains exactly those terms for which one index $k\in I_{j}$ has occupation number one on both sides, while every other index $\ell\in I_{j}\setminus\{k\}$ has occupation number zero on both sides.
	Thus, $\Pi_{j}^{\mathrm{succ}}\ket{\Phi_{j}} = -[\prod_{k\in I_{j}}(1-\zeta_{k})]^{1/2} \sum_{k\in I_{j}}r_{k}\ket{k}_{A}\ket{k}_{B}$.
	Its squared norm therefore gives the reference success probability
	\begin{align}
		p_{j}^{\mathrm{ref}}
		:=\bra{\Phi_{j}}\Pi_{j}^{\mathrm{succ}}\ket{\Phi_{j}}
		=\left(\prod_{k\in I_{j}}(1-\zeta_{k})\right) \sum_{k\in I_{j}}r_{k}^{2}
		=\left(\prod_{k\in I_{j}}(1-\zeta_{k})\right)u_{j}
		\ge (1-2u_{*})u_{*} \ge \frac{u_{*}}{2}. \label{eq:sharpness-reference-success-probability}
	\end{align}
	Here we used $\prod_{k\in I_{j}}(1-\zeta_{k})\ge1-u_{j}$ and $u_{*}\le u_{j}\le2u_{*}$.
	The last inequality follows from $u_{*}=1/(64D^{2\alpha}) \le 1/64$.
	After normalization, the successful reference state is, up to an irrelevant overall phase,
	\begin{align}
		\ket{\psi_{j}^{\mathrm{pair}}}
		:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{u_{j}}}\sum_{k\in I_{j}}r_{k}\ket{k}_{A}\ket{k}_{B}. \label{eq:sharpness-one-pair-output}
	\end{align}
	
	
	
	Let $p_{j}^{\mathrm{succ}}:=\operatorname{Tr}(\rho_{j}\Pi_{j}^{\mathrm{succ}})$ be the success probability for the actual state $\rho_{j}$, and set $t_{j}:=\left \|  \rho_{j}-\ket{\Phi_{j}}\bra{\Phi_{j}} \right \|_{1}/2$.
	Equation~\eqref{eq:sharpness-good-block-distance} gives $t_{j}\le\sqrt{\mathfrak{d}_{j}}$.
	Since $\Pi_{j}^{\mathrm{succ}}$ is a measurement projector, the difference between the actual and reference success probabilities satisfies $|p_{j}^{\mathrm{succ}}-p_{j}^{\mathrm{ref}}|\le t_{j}$.
	Moreover, multiplying the two states in Eq.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-good-block-distance} by $\Pi_{j}^{\mathrm{succ}}$ on both sides cannot increase their trace distance, since $\left \|  \Pi_{j}^{\mathrm{succ}}X\Pi_{j}^{\mathrm{succ}} \right \|_{1}\le\left \|  X \right \|_{1}$ for every operator $X$.
	Using also
	$\Pi_{j}^{\mathrm{succ}}\ket{\Phi_{j}}\bra{\Phi_{j}}\Pi_{j}^{\mathrm{succ}}
	=p_{j}^{\mathrm{ref}}\ket{\psi_{j}^{\mathrm{pair}}}\bra{\psi_{j}^{\mathrm{pair}}}$,
	we obtain
	\begin{align}
		\left \|  \Pi_{j}^{\mathrm{succ}}\rho_{j}\Pi_{j}^{\mathrm{succ}} 		- p_{j}^{\mathrm{ref}}\ket{\psi_{j}^{\mathrm{pair}}}\bra{\psi_{j}^{\mathrm{pair}}} \right \|_{1} = \left \|  \Pi_{j}^{\mathrm{succ}} \left( \rho_{j} - \ket{\Phi_{j}} \bra{\Phi_{j}} \right) \Pi_{j}^{\mathrm{succ}} \right \|_{1}
		\le \left \|  \rho_{j} - \ket{\Phi_{j}} \bra{\Phi_{j}} \right \|_{1} = 2t_{j}.
	\end{align}
	Since $I_{j}$ is good, Eq.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-good-block-distance} gives
	$t_{j}\le\sqrt{\mathfrak{d}_{j}}\le\sqrt{\mathfrak{d}_{*}}=u_{*}\vartheta/8$.
	Combining this with $|p_{j}^{\mathrm{succ}}-p_{j}^{\mathrm{ref}}|\le t_{j}$ and Eq.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-reference-success-probability}, we obtain
	\begin{align}
		p_{j}^{\mathrm{succ}}
		\ge p_{j}^{\mathrm{ref}}-t_{j}
		\ge\frac{u_{*}}{2}-\frac{u_{*}\vartheta}{8}
		\ge\frac{u_{*}}{4}>0, \label{eq:sharpness-actual-success-probability}
	\end{align}
	where the last inequality uses $0 < \vartheta \le 1/24$.
	We may therefore define the normalized successful output by $\rho_{j}^{\mathrm{succ}}:=\Pi_{j}^{\mathrm{succ}}\rho_{j}\Pi_{j}^{\mathrm{succ}}/p_{j}^{\mathrm{succ}}$.
	Using the preceding trace-distance bound, $|p_{j}^{\mathrm{succ}}-p_{j}^{\mathrm{ref}}|\le t_{j}$, and Eq.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-actual-success-probability}, the triangle inequality gives
	\begin{align}
		\frac{1}{2}\left \|  \rho_{j}^{\mathrm{succ}}-\ket{\psi_{j}^{\mathrm{pair}}}\bra{\psi_{j}^{\mathrm{pair}}} \right \|_{1}
		\le \frac{2t_{j}+|p_{j}^{\mathrm{succ}}-p_{j}^{\mathrm{ref}}|}{2p_{j}^{\mathrm{succ}}}
		\le \frac{6t_{j}}{u_{*}}
		\le\frac{8\sqrt{\mathfrak{d}_{j}}}{u_{*}}
		\le\vartheta. \label{eq:sharpness-output-distance}
	\end{align}
	
	
	
	Since the one-particle states $\{\ket{k}_{A}\}_{k\in I_{j}}$ and $\{\ket{k}_{B}\}_{k\in I_{j}}$ are orthonormal, Eq.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-one-pair-output} is already a Schmidt decomposition.
	Its Schmidt coefficients are $r_{k}/\sqrt{u_{j}}$, and hence the eigenvalues of the reduced state on $B$ are
	\begin{align}
		\frac{r_{k}^{2}}{u_{j}}=\frac{\zeta_{k}}{u_{j}},\qquad k\in I_{j}.
	\end{align}
	By Eqs.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-total-squeezing-weight} and \eqref{eq:sharpness-mode-block-weight},
	$\zeta_{k}\le f^{2}$ and $u_{j}\ge u_{*}$, so
	$\max_{k\in I_{j}}\zeta_{k}/u_{j}\le f^{2}/u_{*}$.
	Since the Shannon entropy of a probability distribution is bounded below by minus the logarithm of its largest probability, the entanglement entropy of the pure state $\ket{\psi_{j}^{\mathrm{pair}}}$ satisfies
	\begin{align}
		S((\psi_{j}^{\mathrm{pair}})_{B})
		\ge -\log \left(\max_{k\in I_{j}}\frac{\zeta_{k}}{u_{j}}\right)
		\ge\log\frac{u_{*}}{f^{2}}. \label{eq:sharpness-reference-output-entropy}
	\end{align}
	Equation~\eqref{eq:sharpness-output-distance} shows that $\rho_{j}^{\mathrm{succ}}$ is within trace distance $\vartheta$ of $\ket{\psi_{j}^{\mathrm{pair}}}\bra{\psi_{j}^{\mathrm{pair}}}$.
	By contractivity of the trace distance under the partial trace, their $B$-marginals are also within trace distance $\vartheta$.
	Both $\rho_{j}^{\mathrm{succ}}$ and $\ket{\psi_{j}^{\mathrm{pair}}}\bra{\psi_{j}^{\mathrm{pair}}}$ are supported on the tensor product of the one-particle sectors of the $M_{j}$ rotated modes on the two sides.
	Thus, the local space on $B$ has dimension $M_{j}$, while the joint space has dimension $M_{j}^{2}$.
	Equation~\eqref{eq:sharpness-output-distance}, together with contractivity of the trace distance under the partial trace, allows us to apply Eq.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-continuity} both to the $B$-marginals and to the joint states.
	Since $\ket{\psi_{j}^{\mathrm{pair}}}$ is pure, this gives
	\begin{align}
		S((\rho_{j}^{\mathrm{succ}})_{B})
		&\ge S((\psi_{j}^{\mathrm{pair}})_{B})-\vartheta\log M_{j}-\omega(\vartheta),\notag\\
		S(\rho_{j}^{\mathrm{succ}})
		&\le 2\vartheta\log M_{j}+\omega(\vartheta).
	\end{align}
	Subtracting the second estimate from the first and using Eq.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-reference-output-entropy}, we obtain
	\begin{align}
		S((\rho_{j}^{\mathrm{succ}})_{B})-S(\rho_{j}^{\mathrm{succ}})
		\ge \log\frac{u_{*}}{f^{2}}
		-3\vartheta\log M_{j}
		-2\omega(\vartheta). \label{eq:sharpness-block-coherent-information-pre}
	\end{align}
	Lemma~\ref{lem:sharpness-matrix} gives $f^{2}\lesssim\ell_{\Lambda}^{-(D-\alpha)}$, and hence
	$\log(u_{*}/f^{2})\ge(D-\alpha)\log\ell_{\Lambda}-\mathcal{O}_{D,\alpha}(1)$.
	Moreover, $M_{j}\le N_{\mathrm{h}}\le\ell_{\Lambda}^{D}$ and $3\vartheta D=(D-\alpha)/8$.
	Therefore, Eq.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-block-coherent-information-pre} gives
	\begin{align}
		S((\rho_{j}^{\mathrm{succ}})_{B})-S(\rho_{j}^{\mathrm{succ}})
		\ge\left(D-\alpha-3\vartheta D\right)\log\ell_{\Lambda}-\mathcal{O}_{D,\alpha}(1) 
		=\frac{7(D-\alpha)}{8}\log\ell_{\Lambda}-\mathcal{O}_{D,\alpha}(1) 
		\ge\frac{D-\alpha}{2}\log\ell_{\Lambda} \label{eq:sharpness-block-coherent-information}
	\end{align}
	for all sufficiently large $\ell_{\Lambda}$.
	
	
	Let $A_{I_{j}}$ and $B_{I_{j}}$ denote the rotated modes indexed by $I_{j}$ on the two sides.
	Average LOCC monotonicity in Eq.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-Esq-LOCC}, together with the nonnegativity of squashed entanglement, gives
	\begin{align}
		E_{\mathrm{sq}}(A_{I_{j}}:B_{I_{j}})_{\rho_{j}} \ge p_{j}^{\mathrm{succ}} E_{\mathrm{sq}}(A_{I_{j}}:B_{I_{j}})_{\rho_{j}^{\mathrm{succ}}}.
	\end{align}
	Using the coherent-information lower bound in Eq.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-coherent-info}, together with Eqs.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-actual-success-probability} and \eqref{eq:sharpness-block-coherent-information}, we obtain
	\begin{align}
		E_{\mathrm{sq}}(A_{I_{j}}:B_{I_{j}})_{\rho_{j}} \ge \frac{u_{*}(D-\alpha)}{8} \log \ell_{\Lambda}. \label{eq:sharpness-good-block-Esq}
	\end{align}
	There are at least $W/(32u_{*})$ good index sets.
	Since $\mathsf{J}$ and the mode rotations are local with respect to $A|B$, they preserve the entanglement across this bipartition.
	Using the pure-state identity in Eq.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-Esq-pure} and the strong superadditivity of squashed entanglement in Eq.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-Esq-superadditivity}, we therefore obtain
	\begin{align}
		S_{A}(\ket{\Omega_{F}}) \ge \sum_{j:\,I_{j}\,\mathrm{good}}
		E_{\mathrm{sq}}(A_{I_{j}}:B_{I_{j}})_{\rho_{j}} \ge \frac{W}{32u_{*}}\frac{u_{*}(D-\alpha)}{8}\log \ell_{\Lambda}
		= \frac{D-\alpha}{256}W\log \ell_{\Lambda}.
	\end{align}
	This proves Eq.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-entropy-W}.
<!-- end proof-37 -->
<!-- lemma-21: body_md -->
\label{lem:sharpness-unique}
	Assume $f\le1/2$ and $\mathfrak{R}<1$.
	Then
	\begin{align}
		\Delta_{F}
		\ge\sqrt{1-f^{2}}-\frac{4\mathfrak{R}}{1-\mathfrak{R}}. \label{eq:sharpness-finite-gap}
	\end{align}
	In particular, positivity of the right-hand side proves uniqueness of the spin ground state.
<!-- end lemma-21 -->
<!-- proof-38: body_md -->
The proof compares the two lowest spin energies with the corresponding auxiliary bosonic energies.
	For the spin ground energy, we project the bosonic ground state $\ket{\Phi}$ onto the original-mode hard-core subspace and control the resulting energy cost by $\mathfrak{R}$.
	For the first excited energy, the bosonic gap and the min--max principle give a lower bound directly through the compression to the spin Hilbert space.
	
	
	We first control both the weight of the bosonic ground state outside the hard-core subspace and the energy cost of projecting it onto that subspace.
	For this purpose, define the mean occupations in the original site modes and their total collision weight by
	\begin{align}
		\bar{n}_{A,a}:=\bra{\Phi}a_{a}^{\dagger}a_{a}\ket{\Phi},\qquad
		\bar{n}_{B,b}:=\bra{\Phi}b_{b}^{\dagger}b_{b}\ket{\Phi},\qquad
		\mathfrak{c}:=\sum_{a}\bar{n}_{A,a}^{2}+\sum_{b}\bar{n}_{B,b}^{2}. \label{eq:sharpness-site-occupations}
	\end{align}
	From Eq.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-bosonic-ground-state}, the mean occupation of either rotated mode in the $k$th paired factor is
	$\bar{n}_{k}:=\bra{\phi_{k}}\widehat{a}_{k}^{\dagger}\widehat{a}_{k}\ket{\phi_{k}}
	=\bra{\phi_{k}}\widehat{b}_{k}^{\dagger}\widehat{b}_{k}\ket{\phi_{k}}
	=r_{k}^{2}/(1-r_{k}^{2})$.
	Using $r_{k}=s_{k}/(1+\sqrt{1-s_{k}^{2}})$, this becomes
	$\bar{n}_{k}=[1/\sqrt{1-s_{k}^{2}}-1]/2\le s_{k}^{2}$ for $s_{k}\le1/2$.
	Transforming back to the original site modes using Eq.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-mode-rotations}, and using $\bar{n}_{k}\le s_{k}^{2}$, gives
	\begin{align}
		\bar{n}_{A,a}
		&=\sum_{k}|(U_{F})_{ak}|^{2}\bar{n}_{k}
		\le\sum_{k}|(U_{F})_{ak}|^{2}s_{k}^{2}
		=(FF^{\dagger})_{aa}, \\
		\bar{n}_{B,b}
		&=\sum_{k}|(V_{F})_{bk}|^{2}\bar{n}_{k}
		\le\sum_{k}|(V_{F})_{bk}|^{2}s_{k}^{2}
		=(F^{\dagger}F)_{bb}.
	\end{align}
	Squaring the preceding bounds and summing over the original site modes gives
	\begin{align}
		\mathfrak{c} = \sum_{a}\bar{n}_{A,a}^{2}+\sum_{b}\bar{n}_{B,b}^{2}  \le \sum_{a}(FF^{\dagger})_{aa}^{2}+\sum_{b}(F^{\dagger}F)_{bb}^{2} = \sum_{a}\left(\sum_{b}|F_{ab}|^{2}\right)^{2}
		+\sum_{b}\left(\sum_{a}|F_{ab}|^{2}\right)^{2} = \mathfrak{R}.
	\end{align}
	
	
	To control both violations of the hard-core constraint and the commutators with the hard-core projection, we need the second factorial moment of the bosonic occupation at each original site.
	Write $c_{x_{A}(a)}:=a_{a}$ and $c_{x_{B}(b)}:=b_{b}$, and define the original-mode bosonic number operator $n_{x}^{\mathrm{b}}:=c_{x}^{\dagger}c_{x}$.
	This operator is distinct from the spin projection $n_{x}$ in Eq.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-spin-model}.
	Let $\bar{n}_{x}$ denote $\bar{n}_{A,a}$ or $\bar{n}_{B,b}$ according to the side containing $x$.
	Since $\ket{\Phi}$ is a product of two-mode squeezed Gaussian states, the rotated $A$ modes satisfy
	$\bra{\Phi}\widehat{a}_{k}^{\dagger}\widehat{a}_{\ell}\ket{\Phi} =\bar{n}_{k}\delta_{k\ell}$, while
	$\bra{\Phi}\widehat{a}_{k}\widehat{a}_{\ell}\ket{\Phi}  = \bra{\Phi}\widehat{a}_{k}^{\dagger}\widehat{a}_{\ell}^{\dagger}\ket{\Phi}=0$.
	Hence, Wick's theorem gives
	\begin{align}
		\bra{\Phi}\widehat{a}_{k}^{\dagger}\widehat{a}_{\ell}^{\dagger}\widehat{a}_{m}\widehat{a}_{p}\ket{\Phi}
		=\bar{n}_{k}\bar{n}_{\ell}\left(\delta_{km}\delta_{\ell p}+\delta_{kp}\delta_{\ell m}\right).
	\end{align}
	Substituting $a_{a}=\sum_{k}(U_{F})_{ak}\widehat{a}_{k}$ from Eq.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-mode-rotations} gives
	\begin{align}
		\bra{\Phi}(a_{a}^{\dagger})^{2}a_{a}^{2}\ket{\Phi}
		=2\left(\sum_{k}|(U_{F})_{ak}|^{2}\bar{n}_{k}\right)^{2}
		=2\bar{n}_{A,a}^{2}.
	\end{align}
	The same calculation with $b_{b}=\sum_{k}\overline{(V_{F})_{bk}} \, \widehat{b}_{k}$ gives the corresponding identity on $B$.
	Since $(c_{x}^{\dagger})^{2}c_{x}^{2}=n_{x}^{\mathrm{b}}(n_{x}^{\mathrm{b}}-\mathds{1})$, we obtain
	$\bra{\Phi}n_{x}^{\mathrm{b}}(n_{x}^{\mathrm{b}}-\mathds{1})\ket{\Phi}=2\bar{n}_{x}^{2}$.
	The spectral inequalities
	$\mathds{1}_{[2,\infty)}(n_{x}^{\mathrm{b}})\le n_{x}^{\mathrm{b}}(n_{x}^{\mathrm{b}}-\mathds{1})/2$
	and
	$\mathds{1}_{\{1\}}(n_{x}^{\mathrm{b}})\le n_{x}^{\mathrm{b}}$
	therefore give
	\begin{align}
		\bra{\Phi}\mathds{1}_{[2,\infty)}(n_{x}^{\mathrm{b}})\ket{\Phi}
		\le \bar{n}_{x}^{2},\qquad
		\bra{\Phi}\mathds{1}_{\{2\}}(n_{x}^{\mathrm{b}})\ket{\Phi}
		\le \bar{n}_{x}^{2},\qquad
		\bra{\Phi}\mathds{1}_{\{1\}}(n_{x}^{\mathrm{b}})\ket{\Phi}
		\le \bar{n}_{x}.
		\label{eq:sharpness-occupation-probabilities}
	\end{align}
	
	
	Let $\Pi_{x}^{\mathrm{hc}}:=\ket{0}_{x}\bra{0}_{x}+\ket{1}_{x}\bra{1}_{x}$ and $\Pi_{\mathrm{hc}} := \prod_{x}\Pi_{x}^{\mathrm{hc}}$ be the projection onto the original-mode hard-core subspace.
	A violation of the hard-core constraint requires $n_{x}^{\mathrm{b}}\ge2$ for at least one site $x$, and hence
	\begin{align}
		\mathds{1}-\Pi_{\mathrm{hc}} \le \sum_{x}\mathds{1}_{[2,\infty)}(n_{x}^{\mathrm{b}}).
	\end{align}
	Using Eq.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-occupation-probabilities} and the definition of $\mathfrak{c}$ in Eq.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-site-occupations}, we obtain
	\begin{align}
		1-\left \|  \Pi_{\mathrm{hc}}\ket{\Phi} \right \|^{2}  =\bra{\Phi}(\mathds{1}-\Pi_{\mathrm{hc}})\ket{\Phi}  \le\sum_{x}\bra{\Phi}\mathds{1}_{[2,\infty)}(n_{x}^{\mathrm{b}})\ket{\Phi}  \le\sum_{a}\bar{n}_{A,a}^{2}+\sum_{b}\bar{n}_{B,b}^{2}
		=\mathfrak{c}.
	\end{align}
	Therefore,
	\begin{align}
		\left \|  \Pi_{\mathrm{hc}} \ket{\Phi} \right \|^{2}\ge1-\mathfrak{c}. \label{eq:sharpness-hard-core-weight}
	\end{align}
	
	
	
	We next control the energy increase caused by the hard-core projection.
	To express the Bogoliubov annihilation operators in the original site basis, define
	$R_{F}:=U_{F}\operatorname{diag}(r_{k})V_{F}^{\dagger}$, which is obtained from the singular-value decomposition
	$F=U_{F}\operatorname{diag}(s_{k})V_{F}^{\dagger}$ by replacing each $s_{k}$ with $r_{k}$.
	Then define
	\begin{align}
		X_{a}&:=a_{a}+\sum_{b}(R_{F})_{ab}b_{b}^{\dagger},\qquad
		Y_{b}:=b_{b}+\sum_{a}(R_{F})_{ab}a_{a}^{\dagger}. \label{eq:sharpness-unnormalized-annihilators}
	\end{align}
	Using Eq.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-mode-rotations} and the definition of $R_{F}$, these operators can be written as
	$X_{a}=\sum_{k}(U_{F})_{ak}(\widehat{a}_{k}+r_{k}\widehat{b}_{k}^{\dagger})$
	and
	$Y_{b}=\sum_{k}\overline{(V_{F})_{bk}}(\widehat{b}_{k}+r_{k}\widehat{a}_{k}^{\dagger})$.
	Since the operators in parentheses annihilate the corresponding factors $\ket{\phi_{k}}$ in Eq.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-bosonic-ground-state}, we have
	$X_{a}\ket{\Phi}=Y_{b}\ket{\Phi}=0$.
	Moreover, Eq.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-bosonic-diagonal-block} gives
	\begin{align}
		H_{\mathrm{b}}-E_{\mathrm{b}}\mathds{1}
		=\sum_{k}\frac{\varepsilon_{k}}{1-r_{k}^{2}}
		\left[
		(\widehat{a}_{k}+r_{k}\widehat{b}_{k}^{\dagger})^{\dagger}
		(\widehat{a}_{k}+r_{k}\widehat{b}_{k}^{\dagger})
		+
		(\widehat{b}_{k}+r_{k}\widehat{a}_{k}^{\dagger})^{\dagger}
		(\widehat{b}_{k}+r_{k}\widehat{a}_{k}^{\dagger})
		\right].
	\end{align}
	Since
	$\varepsilon_{k}/(1-r_{k}^{2})=(1+\varepsilon_{k})/2\le1$
	and $U_{F}$ and $V_{F}$ are unitary,
	\begin{align}
		\sum_{k}
		(\widehat{a}_{k}+r_{k}\widehat{b}_{k}^{\dagger})^{\dagger}
		(\widehat{a}_{k}+r_{k}\widehat{b}_{k}^{\dagger})
		&=\sum_{a}X_{a}^{\dagger}X_{a}, \\
		\sum_{k}
		(\widehat{b}_{k}+r_{k}\widehat{a}_{k}^{\dagger})^{\dagger}
		(\widehat{b}_{k}+r_{k}\widehat{a}_{k}^{\dagger})
		&=\sum_{b}Y_{b}^{\dagger}Y_{b}.
	\end{align}
	Therefore,
	\begin{align}
		0\le H_{\mathrm{b}}-E_{\mathrm{b}}\mathds{1}
		\le\sum_{a}X_{a}^{\dagger}X_{a}+\sum_{b}Y_{b}^{\dagger}Y_{b}. \label{eq:sharpness-bosonic-upper-factorization}
	\end{align}
	
	
	
	Because $X_{a}$ and $Y_{b}$ annihilate $\ket{\Phi}$, the energy of the projected state is controlled by their commutators with $\Pi_{\mathrm{hc}}$.
	For any original bosonic annihilation operator $c_{x}$,
	\begin{align}
		[c_{x},\Pi_{\mathrm{hc}}]&=-\sqrt{2}\ket{1}_{x}\bra{2}_{x}\prod_{y\ne x}\Pi_{y}^{\mathrm{hc}}, \qquad 		[c_{x}^{\dagger},\Pi_{\mathrm{hc}}] =\sqrt{2}\ket{2}_{x}\bra{1}_{x}\prod_{y\ne x}\Pi_{y}^{\mathrm{hc}}. \label{eq:sharpness-hard-core-commutators}
	\end{align}
	The first commutator has range inside the hard-core subspace, whereas the second creates exactly one hard-core violation at $x$.
	Thus, the two types of ranges are orthogonal, and the ranges of $[c_{x}^{\dagger},\Pi_{\mathrm{hc}}]$ corresponding to distinct sites $x$ are mutually orthogonal.
	Taking the squared norms in Eq.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-hard-core-commutators} and using that the hard-core projections on the other sites are bounded by the identity gives
	$\left \|  [c_{x},\Pi_{\mathrm{hc}}]\ket{\Phi} \right \|^{2}
	\le2\bra{\Phi}\mathds{1}_{\{2\}}(n_{x}^{\mathrm{b}})\ket{\Phi}$
	and
	$\left \|  [c_{x}^{\dagger},\Pi_{\mathrm{hc}}]\ket{\Phi} \right \|^{2}
	\le2\bra{\Phi}\mathds{1}_{\{1\}}(n_{x}^{\mathrm{b}})\ket{\Phi}$.
	Hence, Eq.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-occupation-probabilities} gives
	$\left \|  [c_{x},\Pi_{\mathrm{hc}}]\ket{\Phi} \right \|^{2}\le2\bar{n}_{x}^{2}$
	and
	$\left \|  [c_{x}^{\dagger},\Pi_{\mathrm{hc}}]\ket{\Phi} \right \|^{2}\le2\bar{n}_{x}$.
	Moreover,
	$(R_{F}^{\dagger}R_{F})_{bb}
	=\sum_{k}|(V_{F})_{bk}|^{2}r_{k}^{2}
	\le\sum_{k}|(V_{F})_{bk}|^{2}r_{k}^{2}/(1-r_{k}^{2})
	=\bar{n}_{B,b}$,
	and similarly
	$(R_{F}R_{F}^{\dagger})_{aa}\le\bar{n}_{A,a}$.
	Using Eq.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-unnormalized-annihilators} and the orthogonality above, we therefore obtain
	\begin{align}
		\sum_{a}\left \|  [X_{a},\Pi_{\mathrm{hc}}]\ket{\Phi} \right \|^{2}
		&\le2\sum_{a}\bar{n}_{A,a}^{2}
		+2\sum_{a,b}|(R_{F})_{ab}|^{2}\bar{n}_{B,b}
		=2\sum_{a}\bar{n}_{A,a}^{2}
		+2\sum_{b}(R_{F}^{\dagger}R_{F})_{bb}\bar{n}_{B,b}
		\le2\mathfrak{c}, \label{eq:sharpness-X-commutator-bound}\\
		\sum_{b}\left \|  [Y_{b},\Pi_{\mathrm{hc}}]\ket{\Phi} \right \|^{2}
		&\le2\sum_{b}\bar{n}_{B,b}^{2}
		+2\sum_{a,b}|(R_{F})_{ab}|^{2}\bar{n}_{A,a}
		=2\sum_{b}\bar{n}_{B,b}^{2}
		+2\sum_{a}(R_{F}R_{F}^{\dagger})_{aa}\bar{n}_{A,a}
		\le2\mathfrak{c}. \label{eq:sharpness-Y-commutator-bound}
	\end{align}
	
	
	
	Since $\operatorname{Ran}\Pi_{\mathrm{hc}}=\operatorname{Ran}\mathsf{J}$, the normalized vector
	$\Pi_{\mathrm{hc}}\ket{\Phi}/\left \|  \Pi_{\mathrm{hc}}\ket{\Phi} \right \|$
	corresponds to a valid normalized spin trial state.
	Before applying the variational principle, Eq.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-bosonic-upper-factorization} and
	$X_{a}\ket{\Phi}=Y_{b}\ket{\Phi}=0$ give
	\begin{align}
		\bra{\Phi}\Pi_{\mathrm{hc}}(H_{\mathrm{b}}-E_{\mathrm{b}}\mathds{1})\Pi_{\mathrm{hc}}\ket{\Phi}
		&\le\sum_{a}\left \|  X_{a}\Pi_{\mathrm{hc}}\ket{\Phi} \right \|^{2}
		+\sum_{b}\left \|  Y_{b}\Pi_{\mathrm{hc}}\ket{\Phi} \right \|^{2}\notag\\
		&=\sum_{a}\left \|  [X_{a},\Pi_{\mathrm{hc}}]\ket{\Phi} \right \|^{2}
		+\sum_{b}\left \|  [Y_{b},\Pi_{\mathrm{hc}}]\ket{\Phi} \right \|^{2}\notag\\
		&\le4\mathfrak{c},
	\end{align}
	where the last inequality follows from Eqs.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-X-commutator-bound} and \eqref{eq:sharpness-Y-commutator-bound}.
	Applying the variational principle to the normalized projected state and using Eq.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-hard-core-weight}, we therefore obtain
	\begin{align}
		E_{\mathrm{s}}-E_{\mathrm{b}}  \le \frac{\bra{\Phi}\Pi_{\mathrm{hc}}(H_{\mathrm{b}}-E_{\mathrm{b}}\mathds{1})\Pi_{\mathrm{hc}}\ket{\Phi}}
		{\left \|  \Pi_{\mathrm{hc}}\ket{\Phi} \right \|^{2}} \le \frac{4\mathfrak{c}}{1-\mathfrak{c}}
		\le\frac{4\mathfrak{R}}{1-\mathfrak{R}}.
		\label{eq:sharpness-ground-energy-projection-bound}
	\end{align}
	
	
	
	We now derive a lower bound on the first excited spin energy.
	By Eq.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-bosonic-diagonal-block}, the unique bosonic ground state is $\ket{\Phi}$ and the first excitation energy above it is $\sqrt{1-f^{2}}$.
	Since $\mathsf{J}$ is an isometry, $H_{F}=\mathsf{J}^{\dagger}H_{\mathrm{b}}\mathsf{J}$ is the compression of $H_{\mathrm{b}}$ to the subspace $\operatorname{Ran}\mathsf{J}$, and the min--max principle gives
	\begin{align}
		E_{1}(H_{F}) \ge E_{1}(H_{\mathrm{b}}) = E_{\mathrm{b}}+\sqrt{1-f^{2}}. \label{eq:sharpness-first-excited-minmax}
	\end{align}
	Subtracting Eq.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-ground-energy-projection-bound} from Eq.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-first-excited-minmax} yields
	\begin{align}
		\Delta_{F}
		=E_{1}(H_{F})-E_{\mathrm{s}}
		\ge\sqrt{1-f^{2}}-\frac{4\mathfrak{R}}{1-\mathfrak{R}},
	\end{align}
	which proves Eq.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-finite-gap}.
<!-- end proof-38 -->
<!-- proof-39: body_md -->
Let $\ket{\Omega_{F}}$ be any normalized pure ground state of Eq.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-spin-model}.
	By Lemma~\ref{lem:sharpness-entropy-from-defect},
	\begin{align}
		S_{A}(\ket{\Omega_{F}})
		\ge\frac{D-\alpha}{256}\,W\log \ell_{\Lambda}. \label{eq:sharpness-final-entropy-combination}
	\end{align}
	Combining Eq.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-final-entropy-combination} with the three regimes for $W$ in Eq.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-HS-bound} gives
	\begin{align}
		S_{A}(\ket{\Omega_{F}})
		\gtrsim
		\begin{cases}
			\log \ell_{\Lambda},&0\le\alpha<(D+1)/2,\\
			(\log \ell_{\Lambda})^{2},&\alpha=(D+1)/2,\\
			\ell_{\Lambda}^{2\alpha-D-1}\log \ell_{\Lambda},&(D+1)/2<\alpha<D.
		\end{cases}
	\end{align}
	Since $n=\ell_{\Lambda}^{D}$, one has $\log \ell_{\Lambda}=D^{-1}\log n$ and $\ell_{\Lambda}^{2\alpha-D-1}=n^{(2\alpha-D-1)/D}$.
	Therefore
	\begin{align}
		S_{A}(\ket{\Omega_{F}})
		\gtrsim
		\begin{cases}
			\log n,&0\le\alpha<(D+1)/2,\\
			(\log n)^{2},&\alpha=(D+1)/2,\\
			n^{(2\alpha-D-1)/D}\log n,&(D+1)/2<\alpha<D,
		\end{cases}
	\end{align}
	which proves Eq.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-main-lower}.
	No uniqueness assumption has been used, so the bound holds for every pure ground state.
	
	Now assume $0\le\alpha<(3D+1)/4$.
	Lemma~\ref{lem:sharpness-matrix} gives $f=o(1)$ and $\mathfrak{R}=o(1)$, so $f\le1/2$ and $\mathfrak{R}<1$ for all sufficiently large even $\ell_{\Lambda}$.
	Lemma~\ref{lem:sharpness-unique} therefore applies, and Eq.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-finite-gap} gives
	\begin{align}
		\Delta_{F} \ge \sqrt{1-f^{2}}-\frac{4\mathfrak{R}}{1-\mathfrak{R}} = 1-o(1). \label{eq:sharpness-final-gap}
	\end{align}
	In particular, the right-hand side is positive for all sufficiently large even $\ell_{\Lambda}$, so the same lemma implies that the ground state is unique.
	The same estimate proves Eq.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-asymptotic-gap}.
<!-- end proof-39 -->
<!-- proof-40: body_md -->
First suppose that $D\ge1$ and $0\le\alpha<D/2$.
	Since $\alpha<D/2<(3D+1)/4$, Proposition~\ref{prop:regular-cut-obstruction} gives a unique ground state for all sufficiently large even $\ell_{\Lambda}$, with
	\begin{align}
		\Delta_{F}\ge1-o(1),\qquad
		S_{A}(\ket{\Omega_{F}})\ge c_{D,\alpha}\log n.
	\end{align}
	In particular, the gap is bounded below by a positive constant for all sufficiently large $\ell_{\Lambda}$.
	On the other hand, Theorem~\ref{thm:single-scale-arbitrary-cut} applies to every bipartition and gives
	\begin{align}
		S_{A}(\ket{\Omega_{F}})\le C_{D,\alpha}\log n.
	\end{align}
	Thus, for the regular codimension-one cut considered here,
	\begin{align}
		c_{D,\alpha}\log n
		\le S_{A}(\ket{\Omega_{F}})
		\le C_{D,\alpha}\log n,
	\end{align}
	so the logarithmic scaling is optimal throughout $0\le\alpha<D/2$.
	
	
	Assume now that $D\ge2$ and $\alpha=(D+1)/2$.
	Since $(D+1)/2<(3D+1)/4$ for $D>1$, Proposition~\ref{prop:regular-cut-obstruction} again gives a unique ground state for all sufficiently large even $\ell_{\Lambda}$ and $\Delta_{F} \ge 1 - o(1)$.
	After increasing the starting size if necessary, one therefore has $\Delta_{F} \ge 1/2$.
	The critical case of Eq.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-main-lower} gives
	\begin{align}
		S_{A}(\ket{\Omega_{F}})
		\ge c_{D}\,(\log n)^{2},
	\end{align}
	which is Eq.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-critical-lower}.
	
	Next suppose that $(D+1)/2<\alpha<(3D+1)/4$.
	Proposition~\ref{prop:regular-cut-obstruction} gives a unique ground state with $\Delta_{F}\ge1-o(1)$ for all sufficiently large even $\ell_{\Lambda}$, and the corresponding case of Eq.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-main-lower} gives
	\begin{align}
		S_{A}(\ket{\Omega_{F}})
		\ge c_{D,\alpha}\,n^{(2\alpha-D-1)/D}\log n. \label{eq:sharpness-corollary-polynomial}
	\end{align}
	Here $(2\alpha-D-1)/D>0$, so the right-hand side grows polynomially in $n$, up to the additional logarithmic factor.
	This proves Eq.~\eqref{eq:sharpness-polynomial-lower}.
	
	For every $\alpha>(D+1)/2$ sufficiently close to $(D+1)/2$, Proposition~\ref{prop:regular-cut-obstruction} gives a unique uniformly gapped example with polynomially growing regular-cut entanglement.
	In contrast, Theorem~\ref{thm:regular-cut-polylog-entropy} gives a universal polylogarithmic entropy bound for regular codimension-one cuts throughout $D/2<\alpha<(D+1)/2$.
	Therefore, $\alpha=(D+1)/2$ is the optimal threshold for such a universal polylogarithmic bound under the present microscopic assumptions.
